Excellent! Now I have all the information needed. Let me create a comprehensive report.

## Translation/i18n System Report

### Summary
The Safecast New Map project uses a **custom JSON-based translation system** built into Go templates and JavaScript. It's NOT using external libraries like i18next or gettext, but instead implements a lightweight in-house solution.

---

### File Locations

**Primary translation file (source of truth):**
- `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/public_html/translations.json` (406KB)
- `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/public_html/translations.json` (406KB - mirror for legacy build)

**HTML files using translations:**
- `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/public_html/map.html` (main map interface)
- `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/public_html/home.html` (home page)

**Go implementation:**
- `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/main.go` (translation loading and language selection)
- `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/main.go` (legacy build)

---

### Translation File Structure

**Format:** Nested JSON with language codes as top-level keys

```json
{
  "ar": {
    "api_example_archive_desc": "...",
    "api_title": "...",
    ...
  },
  "en": {
    "api_example_archive_desc": "...",
    "api_title": "...",
    ...
  },
  "zh": { ... },
  "es": { ... }
  // etc.
}
```

**Statistics:**
- **29 supported language codes:** ar, cs, da, de, el, en, es, fa, fi, fr, he, hi, hu, id, it, ja, ko, ms, nl, no, pl, pt, ru, sv, th, tr, uk, vi, zh
- **136 translation keys** per language (in English)
- **File size:** ~406KB

**Common key prefixes:**
- `api_example_*` - API documentation examples
- `api_*` - API-related text
- `attr_*` - Attribution/footer attributes
- `legend_*` - Map legend items
- `live_marker_*` - Realtime sensor marker popups
- `live_chart_*` - Chart related strings
- `duration_*` - Time duration formatting
- `legal_*` - Legal/terms text
- `license_*` - License information

---

### Backend Implementation (Go)

**Language Detection:**
```go
func getPreferredLanguage(r *http.Request) string
```
- Reads `Accept-Language` HTTP header from browser
- Normalizes language codes (e.g., `zh-cn` → `zh`, `nb-NO` → `no`)
- Falls back to English (`en`) if not supported
- Defaults to English if no header provided

**Translation Loading:**
```go
func loadTranslations(fs embed.FS, filename string) {
    // Loads translations.json at startup
    // Unmarshals into: var translations map[string]map[string]string
    // Structure: [language][key] → value
}
```
- Called once during server startup: `loadTranslations(content, "public_html/translations.json")`
- File is embedded in the binary via `//go:embed public_html/*`
- Panics if file is missing or invalid JSON

**Template Integration:**
```go
// In route handlers (map.html, home.html, etc.)
tmpl := template.Must(template.New("map.html").Funcs(template.FuncMap{
    "translate": func(key string) string {
        if val, ok := translations[lang][key]; ok {
            return val
        }
        return translations["en"][key]  // Fallback
    },
}).ParseFS(content, "public_html/map.html"))
```

**JavaScript Exposure:**
- All translations are marshaled to JSON and embedded in the HTML as a script variable
- Used via `template.JS` to avoid HTML template escaping issues
- Template struct field: `TranslationsJSON template.JS`

---

### Frontend Implementation (JavaScript)

**Translation Object in HTML:**
```javascript
var translations = {{ .TranslationsJSON }};  // Full i18n object
var currentLang = '{{ .Lang }}';              // Current language code
```

**Translation Function:**
```javascript
function translate(key) {
    if (!translations['en']) {
        console.error('English translations not available!');
        return key;
    }
    
    if (translations[currentLang] && translations[currentLang][key]) {
        return translations[currentLang][key];
    } else if (translations['en'][key]) {
        return translations['en'][key];  // Fallback
    }
    return key;  // Return key if not found
}
```

**Usage in JavaScript:**
```javascript
// Dynamic translation
document.querySelector('h1').textContent = translate('api_title');

// In string templates
translate('api_example_' + example.key + '_desc')

// Error messages
alert(translate('error_during_upload'))
```

**Go Template Usage (server-side rendering):**
```html
<title>{{translate "title"}}</title>
```

---

### How to Modify Translations

**To add/update translations:**

1. **Edit the JSON file:**
   ```
   /home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/public_html/translations.json
   ```

2. **Add a new key for all languages:**
   ```json
   {
     "ar": { "new_key": "...", ... },
     "de": { "new_key": "...", ... },
     "en": { "new_key": "English text" },
     ...
   }
   ```

3. **Use in HTML/JS:**
   ```javascript
   // In JavaScript
   document.getElementById('element').textContent = translate('new_key');
   
   // In Go templates (map.html, home.html)
   {{ translate "new_key" }}
   ```

4. **Rebuild the binary:**
   ```bash
   /usr/local/go/bin/go build -o safecast-new-map ./cmd/unified-server/
   ```
   (Translations are embedded, so rebuild is required)

5. **Restart the service:**
   ```bash
   systemctl restart safecast-new-map
   ```

---

### Key Translation Patterns

**1. Dynamic translation with concatenation:**
```javascript
translate('api_example_' + example.key + '_title')
```

**2. Dose category translations:**
```javascript
translate(doseCategory(marker.doseRate))
```
Maps radiation level to legend text like "legend_safe", "legend_attention", "legend_danger", etc.

**3. Duration formatting:**
- Keys: `duration_days`, `duration_hours`, `duration_minutes`, `duration_months`, `duration_weeks`
- Uses `[[count]]` placeholder for pluralization

**4. Fallback chain:**
- First check: current language (`translations[currentLang][key]`)
- Second check: English (`translations['en'][key]`)
- Last resort: return the key itself (useful for debugging)

---

### Important Notes

- **No external i18n library** - Custom implementation keeps dependencies minimal
- **Translations embedded in binary** - No runtime file loading required
- **Server-side language detection** - Uses `Accept-Language` header, not client-side detection
- **Dual files** - Both `/public_html/translations.json` and `/cmd/unified-server/public_html/translations.json` must stay in sync
- **Missing translations don't break** - Falls back to English or returns the key name
- **All 136+ keys must exist in all languages** - No partial translations; English is the baseline

### How to fix a translation

1. **Edit** [cmd/unified-server/public_html/translations.json](cmd/unified-server/public_html/translations.json) — find the language code (e.g. `"ja"`, `"de"`, `"fr"`) and update the incorrect value for the relevant key.

2. **Rebuild** the binary (translations are embedded):
   ```bash
   /usr/local/go/bin/go build -o safecast-new-map ./cmd/unified-server/
   ```

3. **Restart** the server to pick up changes.

### Structure

```json
{
  "en": { "legend_safe": "Safe", "title": "Safecast Map", ... },
  "ja": { "legend_safe": "安全", "title": "セーフキャストマップ", ... },
  ...
}
```

The fallback chain is: current language → English → raw key name.

legal_full:
  EN: Preamble. We are building an open map where people around the globe share dosimeter readings for the common good — science, ecology, education, and safety. By publishing your data you help many. Please treat this shared work with care.

1) Responsibility. The accuracy and content of any information you submit remain your responsibility. Data are published and used “as is”. The service does not confirm or guarantee their correctness, completeness, or suitability for any purpose and bears no responsibility for any possible outcome of their use.

2) Openness and license. By sharing dosimetry readings, dates, approximate locations, device models, or other facts, you understand they become available to everyone and may be used freely under the CC0 1.0 (Public Domain) license for data, while the code remains available under the MIT license. Authorship stays with you; no compensation is provided; further redistribution by third parties is beyond our control.

3) “As is” and no verification. Publications appear without prior review. We cannot guarantee instrument calibration or the absence of mistakes. The information is shared for research and is not a professional recommendation.

4) Privacy and moderation. To keep the map safe and trustworthy, timestamps and coordinates may be generalized, and technical metadata may be removed or anonymized. We may hide or delete materials that, in our reasonable opinion, are spam, falsified, unlawful, or disruptive. We handle bona fide measurements with care and strive to preserve them.

5) Cookies. The site relies on a short-lived technical session cookie; it disappears when your visit ends. We do not keep any other traces.

Friends, this map is the result of shared effort and open hearts. Please treat it as a sketch of the terrain, not a precise blueprint. If our work resonates with you, join in — together we can make it better.
  CS: Preambule. Společně tvoříme otevřenou mapu, na níž lidé z celého světa sdílejí dozimetrická měření pro dobro všech – pro vědu, životní prostředí, vzdělávání i bezpečnost. Když zveřejníte svá data, pomáháte mnoha lidem. Prosíme, pečujte o toto společné dílo.

1) Odpovědnost. Za správnost a obsah zasílaných informací nesete odpovědnost vy sami. Data se zveřejňují a používají „tak, jak jsou“. Služba nepotvrzuje ani negarantuje jejich správnost, úplnost či vhodnost pro jakýkoli účel a neodpovídá za následky jejich využití.

2) Otevřenost a licence. Sdílením měření, dat, přibližné polohy, modelu zařízení nebo dalších údajů berete na vědomí, že budou dostupné všem a lze je svobodně používat podle licence CC0 1.0 (Public Domain). Kód zůstává pod licencí MIT. Autorství vám zůstává; odměna se neposkytuje; další šíření třetími stranami nemáme pod kontrolou.

3) „Jak jsou“ a bez ověření. Příspěvky se zveřejňují bez předchozí kontroly. Nemůžeme zaručit kalibraci přístrojů ani bezchybnost údajů. Informace slouží k výzkumným účelům a nepředstavují odborné doporučení.

4) Soukromí a moderace. Kvůli bezpečnosti a důvěře můžeme časy a souřadnice zobecňovat a technická metadata odstraňovat či anonymizovat. Materiály, které jsou podle našeho rozumného uvážení spam, padělek, porušují zákon nebo narušují službu, můžeme skrýt či smazat. Poctivých měření si vážíme a usilujeme o jejich zachování.

5) Cookies. Web používá pouze krátkodobé technické session cookie; po skončení návštěvy zmizí. Žádné další stopy neukládáme.

Přátelé, tato mapa je výsledkem společného úsilí a otevřených srdcí. Vnímejte ji jako náčrt krajiny, nikoli jako přesný plán. Pokud vám naše práce dává smysl, přidejte se – společně ji vylepšíme.

Zelená (0–11 µR/h)
Téměř přirozené pozadí.
• Voda ze studní je obvykle bezpečná.
• Rostliny lze pěstovat bez testů.

Žlutá (11–30 µR/h)
Zvýšené pozadí.
• Zkontrolujte vodu a půdu.
• Otestujte jakékoli potraviny před konzumací.

Červená (30–100 µR/h)
Vážná kontaminace.
• Nepijte vodu.
• Pěstování nebo konzumace je riskantní; laboratorní testy jsou nutné.

Černá (>100 µR/h)
Kritická zóna.
• Voda ani jídlo nejsou použitelné.
• Zůstávejte jen krátce a s ochranou.

legend_full_en:
  EN: <h1>📍 How to read a radiation map</h1><section style="margin:0 0 12px;"><h2>The key points from the outset</h2><ul style="margin:6px 0 0 18px;"><li>The number shown on the map reflects <strong>only part of the radiation hazard</strong> — the portion the instrument can detect.</li><li>Real harm is more often caused <strong>not by sudden spikes</strong> but by <strong>quiet, long-term exposure</strong> that builds up over years.</li><li>Even in a "green" area, risk may still exist due to <strong>invisible sources</strong>: radon, water, food and air.</li><li>A map is <strong>a guide, not a guarantee</strong>. It helps you think but <strong>does not replace common sense</strong>.</li></ul></section><hr /><section style="margin:0 0 12px; display:flex; gap:10px; align-items:flex-start;"><div style="min-width:84px; padding:8px 10px; border-radius:10px; background:#1b5e20; color:#fff; text-align:center; font-weight:700;">🟩<br />0–11</div><div><h2 style="margin:0;">Green zone</h2><h3 style="margin:4px 0; font-weight:600;">"Generally safe — but stay attentive"</h3><p style="margin:4px 0;"><strong>0–11 microroentgens per hour</strong> — close to natural background.</p><ul style="margin:4px 0 0 18px;"><li>you can usually live here permanently</li><li>drink the water</li><li>grow and eat local produce</li><li>this <strong>does not mean radiation is absent</strong></li><li>it means levels are <strong>about what the body is used to</strong></li><li>⚠ hidden risks still exist: indoor radon; radioactive impurities in water; accumulation of radionuclides in food and tobacco</li></ul></div></section><hr /><section style="margin:0 0 12px; display:flex; gap:10px; align-items:flex-start;"><div style="min-width:84px; padding:8px 10px; border-radius:10px; background:#f9a825; color:#111; text-align:center; font-weight:700;">🟨<br />11–30</div><div><h2 style="margin:0;">Yellow zone</h2><h3 style="margin:4px 0; font-weight:600;">"Pay attention and find out why"</h3><p style="margin:4px 0;"><strong>11–30 microroentgens per hour</strong>. Usually no immediate danger, but a yellow patch inside green is <strong>a reason to pause</strong>.</p><ul style="margin:4px 0 0 18px;"><li>possible causes: radon; alpha or beta contamination the dosimeter only partially sees; localised sources</li><li>sensible steps: test water and food; identify the source; measure indoor radon</li><li>harm <strong>can build up over time</strong>, even if you feel fine now</li></ul></div></section><hr /><section style="margin:0 0 12px; display:flex; gap:10px; align-items:flex-start;"><div style="min-width:84px; padding:8px 10px; border-radius:10px; background:#c62828; color:#fff; text-align:center; font-weight:700;">🟥<br />30–100</div><div><h2 style="margin:0;">Red zone</h2><h3 style="margin:4px 0; font-weight:600;">"Without checks, it is not worth the risk"</h3><p style="margin:4px 0;"><strong>30–100 microroentgens per hour</strong> — no longer a normal level.</p><ul style="margin:4px 0 0 18px;"><li>avoid: living here permanently; regularly drinking local water; relying on local food</li><li>possible causes: man-made radionuclides; contaminated soil; high radon in homes and water</li><li>recommended: drink water <strong>only after testing</strong>; eat food <strong>after checks</strong>; live here <strong>only with full awareness of the risks</strong></li></ul></div></section><hr /><section style="margin:0 0 12px; display:flex; gap:10px; align-items:flex-start;"><div style="min-width:84px; padding:8px 10px; border-radius:10px; background:#111; color:#fff; text-align:center; font-weight:700;">⬛<br />100+</div><div><h2 style="margin:0;">Black zone</h2><h3 style="margin:4px 0; font-weight:600;">"Extended exposure is unsafe"</h3><p style="margin:4px 0;"><strong>Over 100 microroentgens per hour</strong> — a serious level of exposure.</p><ul style="margin:4px 0 0 18px;"><li>risk fronts: external irradiation; radionuclides entering the body; potential skin damage</li><li>food and water may contain hazardous substances even if they look and smell normal</li><li>🔹 exceptions (brief skin exposure): air travel; high-altitude locations</li></ul></div></section><hr /><section style="margin:0 0 12px;"><h2>🔍 What a dosimeter shows — and what it misses</h2><div style="display:flex; gap:12px;"><div style="flex:1;"><h3 style="margin:4px 0;">Typically detected:</h3><ul style="margin:4px 0 0 18px;"><li>gamma radiation</li><li>sometimes part of beta radiation</li></ul></div><div style="flex:1;"><h3 style="margin:4px 0;">Often missed:</h3><ul style="margin:4px 0 0 18px;"><li>alpha radiation (especially dangerous once inside the body)</li><li>radon — a gas we breathe and drink with water</li><li>tritium in water and air</li><li>radionuclides in food</li><li>neutrons (rare, but extremely dangerous)</li><li>👉 a "green" reading <strong>does not equal full safety</strong>.</li></ul></div></div></section><hr /><section style="margin:0 0 12px;"><h2>🧬 How radiation actually enters the body</h2><div style="display:flex; gap:12px;"><div style="flex:1;"><h3 style="margin:4px 0;">The most dangerous routes are quiet and long</h3><ul style="margin:4px 0 0 18px;"><li><strong>Through breathing</strong> — radon, dust, smoke → lungs</li><li><strong>Through water</strong> — tritium and dissolved isotopes → whole body</li><li><strong>Through food</strong> — mushrooms, greens, milk → accumulation in tissues</li><li><strong>Through smoking</strong> — tobacco readily absorbs radionuclides</li></ul></div><div style="flex:1;"><h3 style="margin:4px 0;">Less dangerous:</h3><ul style="margin:4px 0 0 18px;"><li>brief external exposure</li><li>one-off contact</li></ul></div></div></section><hr /><section style="margin:0 0 12px;"><h2>⏳ Time is the main risk factor</h2><p style="margin:4px 0;">Radiation is dangerous <strong>because of prolonged exposure</strong>. Moderate doses taken continuously can be more harmful than a short spike. The body can eliminate radionuclides, but with constant exposure and other stresses those systems <strong>get overloaded</strong>.</p></section><hr /><section style="margin:0 0 12px;"><h2>👥 Who is affected more severely</h2><p style="margin:4px 0;">Higher risk for: older people; those with chronic illnesses. What one body tolerates without consequences may cause serious problems for another.</p></section><hr /><section style="margin:0 0 12px;"><h2>⚛️ Radioactive "disguise"</h2><p style="margin:4px 0;">Some radionuclides resemble ordinary substances to the body: <strong>tritium</strong> — like water; <strong>carbon-14</strong> — like normal carbon; <strong>potassium-40</strong> — like potassium; <strong>strontium, radium</strong> — like calcium. Cells <strong>do not recognise the substitution</strong> and incorporate them. Damage and mutations are possible even when the dosimeter reads "normal."</p></section><hr /><section style="margin:0;"><h2>🗺️ Why a map should not be trusted blindly</h2><p style="margin:4px 0;">Even a perfectly green map reflects <strong>only part of reality</strong>. Hidden factors are not shown. 👉 A map is a hint, not a pardon. You still need to think, check, and ask questions yourself.</p></section>
  CS: This scale shows how likely a spot is safe for folk, water, and food.
Mind: the readings might not be complete, and some rays could be higher or hiding. Treat these numbers as guidance only.

Green (0–11 µR/h)
Background near natural.
• Water from wells is generally safe.
• You can grow plants without checks.

Yellow (11–30 µR/h)
Raised background; take care.
• Check water and soil.
• Test any veg, mushrooms, or other produce before you eat.

Red (30–100 µR/h)
Serious contamination.
• Don't drink the water.
• Growing or eating produce from here is risky; lab tests are a must.

Black (>100 µR/h)
Critical zone.
• Water and food cannot be used.
• Staying long-term is out; only short visits with protection.

sources_full:
  EN: We thank all who share measurements.

Anonymous uploads trace quiet paths on the map.
<a href="https://safecast.org" target="_blank">Safecast</a> fosters a global archive of readings.
<a href="https://atomfast.net" target="_blank">Atomfast</a> keeps the Atomcloud alight.
<a href="https://radiaverse.com" target="_blank">Radioverse</a> gathers insights from Radiacode.

Each contribution widens the common picture; you are warmly invited to add your own.
  CS: Všem, kdo sdílejí měření, děkujeme.

Anonymní nahrávky kreslí na mapě tiché stopy.
<a href="https://safecast.org" target="_blank">Safecast</a> pečuje o celosvětový archiv měření.
<a href="https://atomfast.net" target="_blank">Atomfast</a> udržuje službu Atomcloud v chodu.
<a href="https://radiaverse.com" target="_blank">Radioverse</a> shromažďuje poznatky z Radiacode.

Každý příspěvek rozšiřuje společný obraz; budeme rádi, když přidáte ten svůj.

Here are all the Czech (`cs`) translations side-by-side with English. A few things stand out:

### Obvious issues

1. **`description`** — `"Chichina mapa radiace"` — this looks like a placeholder/joke, not a proper translation. Should probably be `"Safecast Nová Mapa"` or `"Nová mapa Safecast"`.

2. **`title`** — `"Chichina mapa izotopů — Radiologická mapa planety Země"` — same "Chichina" issue. Should be something like `"Safecast Nová Mapa — Radiologická mapa planety Země"`.

3. **`count_rate`** — `"Početní rychlost"` — unusual phrasing. More natural Czech would be `"Četnost impulzů"` or simply `"Počet impulzů"`.

4. **`legend_full_cs`** — written in English rather than Czech (the short version). The longer `legend_full_en` has no Czech equivalent.

5. **Many `live_marker_*` and `live_chart_*` keys are left in English** — not translated at all (e.g., "Close", "All readings", "Humidity", "Device ID", etc.).

6. **`duration_*` keys** — all still in English (`[[count]] days`, `[[count]] hr`, etc.).

### Would you like me to:
- **Fix just the clearly wrong ones** (like "Chichina")?
- **Translate all the untranslated English strings** into Czech?
- **Both?**

If you have a native Czech speaker's corrections for specific strings, I can apply those directly too.

> AGENT

They're used in the `doseCategory()` function at [map.html:3223](cmd/unified-server/public_html/map.html#L3223). It classifies radiation dose rates into safety categories:

- **`legend_safe`** — dose rate up to 0.11 µSv/h
- **`legend_attention`** — 0.11 to 0.30 µSv/h  
- **`legend_danger`** — above 0.30 µSv/h

The returned key is then passed through `translate()` to show the localized label in the realtime sensor popups. In French, "Attention" and "Danger" happen to be spelled identically to English, so those are correct as-is.

> AGENT

Actually it's the opposite of what you're asking. Looking at [map.html:4781-4783](cmd/unified-server/public_html/map.html#L4781-L4783):

```js
const statusHtml = (typeof currentColoring !== 'undefined' && currentColoring === 'chicha')
  ? ` (<a href="#" class="risk-link">${translate(doseCategory(marker.doseRate))}</a>)`
  : '';
```

The `doseCategory` label is shown **only when** `currentColoring === 'chicha'` (Safecast style mode). When using a different color scheme, `statusHtml` is an empty string.

The other usage at [line 4678](cmd/unified-server/public_html/map.html#L4678) is in the realtime sensor popup, which always shows the status label regardless of coloring mode.

So it's used in two places:
1. **Realtime sensor popups** — always shown
2. **Regular marker popups** — only in Safecast/chicha coloring mode

> AGENT

Yes! There's already a `?lang=` URL parameter supported. At [map.html:8271](cmd/unified-server/public_html/map.html#L8271) you can see it reads `lang` from the URL and overrides the browser's language.

Just add `?lang=cs` (or any language code) to the URL:

```
https://simplemap.safecast.org/?lang=cs
https://simplemap.safecast.org/?lang=ja
https://simplemap.safecast.org/?lang=de
```

Note: this overrides the **client-side** JavaScript translations. The server-side rendered parts (page title, meta tags) still use the browser's `Accept-Language` header via `getPreferredLanguage()`. So for a full override you'd also want to change your browser language — but most visible UI text is client-side, so `?lang=` covers the majority.

## Translation Status Report: Legal/License/Sources/Legend Content

### Summary

I've analyzed the `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/public_html/translations.json` file for the completeness of legal, licensing, sources, and legend translations across all 28 languages.

---

### 1. **legal_full, license_full, sources_full Status**

These three keys exist as non-language-specific keys in the translations file (no `_XX` suffix). All languages have these three text strings, but translation completeness varies:

#### **legal_full** (Detailed 5-point legal terms — 1,892 chars in English)
- **24 languages with adequate translations (≥80%):** cs, da, de, el, en, es, fa, fi, fr, hi, hu, id, it, ms, nl, no, pl, pt, ru, sv, th, tr, uk, vi
- **4 languages with SHORT translations (<50%):**
  - **zh (Chinese):** 593 chars (31%)
  - **ja (Japanese):** 836 chars (44%)
  - **ar (Arabic):** 1,499 chars (79%) — borderline adequate
  - **ko (Korean):** 937 chars (50%) — borderline

#### **license_full** (MIT + CC0 licensing — 348 chars in English)
- **25 languages with adequate translations (≥80%):** ar, cs, da, de, el, en, es, fa, fi, fr, hi, hu, id, it, ms, nl, no, pl, pt, ru, sv, th, tr, uk, vi
- **1 language with SHORT translation:**
  - **zh (Chinese):** 153 chars (44%)

#### **sources_full** (Data sources acknowledgement — 454 chars in English)
- **26 languages with adequate translations (≥80%):** ar, cs, da, de, el, en, es, fa, fi, fr, he, hi, hu, id, it, ms, nl, no, pl, pt, ru, sv, th, tr, uk, vi
- **2 languages with NO sources_full at all:**
  - **ja (Japanese):** Missing
  - **ko (Korean):** Missing

---

### 2. **legend_full_XX Keys Status**

The radiation safety legend exists as **language-specific keys** (`legend_full_ar`, `legend_full_cs`, etc.). This is a **comprehensive HTML radiation safety guide** (7,178 chars in English).

#### **All legend_full_XX languages found:**
ar, cs, da, de, el, en, es, fa, fi, fr, he, hi, hu, id, it, ja, ko, ms, nl, no, pl, pt, ru, sv, th, tr, uk, vi, zh (29 total including English)

#### **Translation depth comparison:**
- **English (legend_full_en):** 7,178 chars — Full HTML formatted guide with detailed explanations
- **All other languages:** 230–725 chars (3–10% of English length) — **These are SHORT STUBS**

The non-English legend translations are heavily abbreviated bullet-point versions with only the color-coded safety levels and brief descriptions. None reach 50% of the English guide's length.

**Shortest translations:**
- **zh, ja, ko:** ~230–300 chars (3–4% of English)
- **th, he, ar:** ~437–485 chars (6–7% of English)
- **Most European languages:** ~550–730 chars (8–10% of English)

---

### 3. **Language Completeness Summary**

| Language | legal_full | license_full | sources_full | legend_full_XX | Status |
|----------|-----------|-------------|-------------|---|---------|
| **ar** (Arabic) | ✓ adequate | ✓ adequate | ✓ adequate | STUB (7%) | **Mostly OK** |
| **cs** (Czech) | ✓ adequate | ✓ adequate | ✓ adequate | STUB (10%) | **Mostly OK** |
| **da–ru** (European langs) | ✓ adequate | ✓ adequate | ✓ adequate | STUB (8–10%) | **Mostly OK** |
| **fa** (Farsi) | ✓ adequate | ✓ adequate | ✓ adequate | STUB (8%) | **Mostly OK** |
| **he** (Hebrew) | ✓ adequate | ✓ adequate | ✓ adequate | STUB (7%) | **Mostly OK** |
| **ja** (Japanese) | SHORT (44%) | SHORT (58%) | MISSING | STUB (4%) | **Needs work** |
| **ko** (Korean) | BORDERLINE (50%) | ✓ adequate | MISSING | STUB (4%) | **Needs work** |
| **zh** (Chinese) | SHORT (31%) | SHORT (44%) | ✓ adequate | STUB (3%) | **Needs work** |

---

### 4. **Key Findings**

1. **legal_full & license_full:** Almost all languages have proper full translations. Only East Asian languages (zh, ja, ko) have notably short versions.

2. **sources_full:** Well-translated in 26/28 languages. Only Japanese and Korean are missing this entirely.

3. **legend_full_XX:** This is the **major gap** — English has a comprehensive 7,178-character HTML guide, while all 28 other languages have only abbreviated plain-text bullet-point versions (230–725 chars, or 3–10% of English).

4. **Best translations:** Czech, Danish, Dutch, French, German, Italian, Polish, Portuguese, Spanish, Swedish — all have ≥80% completeness across all three main keys.

5. **Weakest translations:** Chinese, Japanese, Korean. These need:
   - Expanded legal_full and license_full texts
   - Missing sources_full entries (ja, ko)
   - Much more comprehensive legend_full guides (currently just bare stubs)

Good, `home.html` does use Go templates. Now I have the full picture. Here is the comprehensive plan:

---

## Comprehensive i18n Implementation Plan for Safecast New Map

### Current Architecture Summary

**What already works:**
- `translations.json` (427KB, 136 English keys, 29 languages) is loaded at startup into `var translations map[string]map[string]string`
- `map.html` and `home.html` are processed through Go `template.FuncMap` with a `translate` function and receive `TranslationsJSON` for client-side JS usage
- Client-side `translate(key)` function available in `map.html` at line 1645
- `getPreferredLanguage(r)` parses `Accept-Language` header, `?lang=` param overrides on client

**What does NOT have i18n support:**
- `profile.html`, `reset-password.html`, `admin-users.html`, `admin-mcp.html`, `admin-realtime.html` -- all served as raw `embed.FS` bytes with NO template processing
- `api-usage.html` -- also raw bytes, but already uses `translate` in Go template form (served via `home.html` handler pattern? Let me verify...)
- `cmd/web-chat/index.html` -- separate binary, no translations at all
- `cmd/mcp-server/tool_*.go` -- 17 tool files with hardcoded English descriptions
- AI Assistant widget in `map.html` (lines 10100-10490) -- hardcoded English strings in HTML and JS
- Login/Register/Forgot Password modals in `map.html` (lines 2142-2252) -- hardcoded English
- Search bar placeholder, spectrum modal labels -- hardcoded English

---

### Phase 1: Fix Incomplete Long-Form Translations (PR 1)
**Priority: HIGH -- user-facing content, no code changes needed**
**Estimated new keys: 0 (updates to existing keys only)**
**File size impact: ~25KB**

**Work items:**
1. Update `legal_full` for `zh` (currently 593 chars vs 1892 English), `ja` (836), `ko` (937) -- expand to full matching translations
2. Update `license_full` for `zh` (currently 153 chars vs 348 English)
3. Add `sources_full` for `ja` and `ko` (currently present but short at 329/352 vs 454 English -- verify these are actually incomplete vs just shorter due to language)
4. All work in `cmd/unified-server/public_html/translations.json`

**Note:** This is pure translation content work. The `legal_full` for zh at 31% of English length clearly indicates truncation. The ja and ko versions are more borderline but should be reviewed.

---

### Phase 2: Legend Full Expansion (PR 2)
**Priority: HIGH -- critical safety information**
**Estimated new keys: 0 (updates to 28 existing `legend_full_XX` keys)**
**File size impact: ~196KB (28 languages * ~7000 chars each)**

**Work items:**
1. The English `legend_full_en` is 7,178 chars of detailed HTML safety guide
2. All 28 other `legend_full_XX` keys are 230-725 char plain text stubs
3. Each needs expansion to match the English detail level with proper HTML formatting
4. The per-language `legend_full_XX` keys already exist in the right structure -- each language section has both `legend_full_en` (a 713-char English fallback) and `legend_full_XX` (the native stub)

**File size concern:** translations.json grows from 427KB to ~623KB. This is still reasonable for a single embedded JSON file. Consider whether to split legend content into a separate file if it proves problematic.

**Architecture decision:** Keep in `translations.json` for now. The entire file is loaded once at startup and cached in memory. The 623KB is acceptable.

---

### Phase 3: AI Assistant Widget i18n (PR 3)
**Priority: HIGH -- user-facing, most visible new feature**
**Estimated new keys: ~15 * 29 languages**
**File size impact: ~15KB**

**Strings to extract from `map.html` lines 10100-10490:**

| Current hardcoded string | Proposed key |
|---|---|
| "Safecast AI Assistant" | `ai_title` |
| "AI-powered map and data guide" | `ai_subtitle` |
| "Search a place or ask AI..." | `ai_placeholder` |
| "Expand panel" / "Collapse panel" | `ai_expand` / `ai_collapse` |
| "Close panel" | `ai_close` |
| "Teleport map to location" | `ai_search_map` |
| "Send message" | `ai_send` |
| "I have access to live readings..." | `ai_welcome` |
| "...thinking..." | `ai_thinking` |
| "Error: " | `ai_error_prefix` |
| "Connection error: " | `ai_connection_error` |
| "Note: AI-generated response..." (disclaimer) | `ai_disclaimer` |
| "Open AI Assistant" (aria-label) | `ai_open` |

**Implementation approach:**
- These strings are in the `<script>` block that runs client-side, so they need the JS `translate()` function
- The `translate()` function is already available globally in `map.html` at line 1645
- Replace hardcoded strings with `translate('ai_title')` etc.
- For HTML attributes like `placeholder` and `title`, set them from JS on page load
- Add all 15 keys to `translations.json` for all 29 languages

---

### Phase 4: Login/Register/Forgot Password Modals (PR 4)
**Priority: HIGH -- user-facing**
**Estimated new keys: ~30 * 29 languages**
**File size impact: ~25KB**

**Strings to extract from `map.html` lines 2142-2260:**

| String | Proposed key |
|---|---|
| "Login to Safecast" | `auth_login_title` |
| "Email:" | `auth_email_label` |
| "Password:" | `auth_password_label` |
| "your@email.com" | `auth_email_placeholder` |
| "Password" (placeholder) | `auth_password_placeholder` |
| "OR" | `auth_or_divider` |
| "API Key:" | `auth_apikey_label` |
| "Your API key" | `auth_apikey_placeholder` |
| "You can log in with either..." | `auth_apikey_hint` |
| "Login" (button) | `auth_login_button` |
| "Forgot password?" | `auth_forgot_link` |
| "First time? Set up your password" | `auth_first_time` |
| "Don't have an account? Register" | `auth_no_account` |
| "Close" | `auth_close` |
| "Create Account" | `auth_register_title` |
| "Username (optional):" | `auth_username_label` |
| "At least 8 characters" | `auth_password_hint` |
| "Confirm Password:" | `auth_confirm_password` |
| "Re-enter password" | `auth_confirm_placeholder` |
| "Register" (button) | `auth_register_button` |
| "Already have an account? Login" | `auth_has_account` |
| "Reset Password" | `auth_reset_title` |
| "Enter your email address..." | `auth_reset_description` |
| "Send Reset Link" | `auth_reset_button` |

| "Profile" | `auth_profile_link` |
| "Logout" | `auth_logout_button` |
| "Login failed" | `auth_login_error` |

**Implementation approach:**
- These are in the HTML body of `map.html` which goes through Go template processing
- Use `{{translate "auth_login_title"}}` for static text in HTML
- For JS-set text (error messages from fetch handlers at lines 2345-2580), use `translate('auth_login_error')`

---

### Phase 5: Search Bar and Spectrum Modals (PR 5)
**Priority: MEDIUM -- user-facing but less critical**
**Estimated new keys: ~25 * 29 languages**
**File size impact: ~20KB**

**Strings to extract:**
- Search placeholder: "Search for a city or location..." (line 1710)
- Spectrum modal: "Gamma Spectrum" (line 2068), "Energy Spectrum" (line 2076), "Download JSON", "Download CSV" (lines 2091-2092), "Close" (line 2094)
- Coordinate input dialog: "Set Spectrum Location" (line 2101), "This spectrum file doesn't contain GPS coordinates..." (line 2103)
- Location form labels and buttons in the spectrum coordinate dialog

**Implementation:** Same pattern as Phase 4 -- Go templates for HTML, JS `translate()` for dynamic text.

---

### Phase 6: Enable Template Processing for profile.html and reset-password.html (PR 6)
**Priority: MEDIUM -- user-facing pages**
**Estimated new keys: ~25 (profile) + ~10 (reset-password) * 29 languages**
**File size impact: ~20KB**

**Current problem:** These pages are served as raw bytes at lines 8838-8856 of `main.go`:
```go
data, err := content.ReadFile("public_html/profile.html")
w.Write(data)
```

**Required changes to `cmd/unified-server/main.go`:**
1. Change the profile handler to use `template.Must(template.New("profile.html").Funcs(template.FuncMap{...}).ParseFS(content, "public_html/profile.html"))` -- same pattern as `map.html` and `home.html` handlers
2. Pass `Lang` and `TranslationsJSON` to the template data
3. Same for `reset-password.html`
4. Add `translate` FuncMap to both handlers
5. Replace hardcoded strings in both HTML files with `{{translate "key"}}` calls and add a `<script>` block with `var translations = {{.TranslationsJSON}}` for client-side JS

**Profile page strings (~25):** "My Profile", "Back to Map", section headers, form labels, buttons, table headers, success/error messages

**Reset password strings (~10):** "Reset Your Password", form labels, buttons, messages

---

### Phase 7: Web-Chat Standalone Page (PR 7)
**Priority: MEDIUM -- separate binary, user-facing**
**Estimated new keys: ~20 * 29 languages**
**File size impact: ~15KB (in a separate or shared translation file)**

**Current state:** `cmd/web-chat/main.go` embeds `index.html` as raw bytes and serves it directly. No template processing, no translations.

**Strings to extract from `cmd/web-chat/index.html`:**
- "Safecast Radiation Assistant" (title + h1)
- "Radiation data from the Safecast sensor network"
- "Download"
- "Ask about radiation data"
- "I have access to live readings from Safecast sensors..."
- 5 quick suggestion chips (these are language-specific prompts)
- "Ask about radiation levels, sensors, or locations..."
- "Send" (button title)
- Disclaimer text
- "Connection error:"
- "...thinking..."
- Copy/download button labels

**Architecture decision -- how to get translations into web-chat:**

**Option A: Embed a copy of translations.json in web-chat**
- Pro: Self-contained binary, no runtime dependency
- Con: Duplicated 600KB+ file, must keep in sync

**Option B: web-chat fetches translations from the main server at runtime**
- Pro: Single source of truth
- Con: Extra HTTP request, dependency on main server

**Option C: Extract shared translation keys into a smaller web-chat-translations.json**
- Pro: Small file, only what's needed (~20 keys * 29 langs = ~30KB)
- Con: Two translation files to maintain

**Recommendation: Option C.** Create `cmd/web-chat/translations.json` with only the ~20 keys needed. Convert `cmd/web-chat/main.go` to use Go template processing (like the main server) with `getPreferredLanguage` from Accept-Language header. Embed the small translations file alongside `index.html`.

**Implementation:**
1. Add `//go:embed translations.json` to `cmd/web-chat/main.go`
2. Add `getPreferredLanguage(r)` function (copy from main server, or extract to a shared internal package)
3. Process `index.html` through `template.Execute` with translate FuncMap
4. Add `<script>var translations = {{.TranslationsJSON}}</script>` for client-side JS translation
5. Replace hardcoded strings

---

### Phase 8: Admin Pages (PR 8)
**Priority: LOW -- admin-only, typically English-speaking audience**
**Estimated new keys: ~100 * 29 languages**
**File size impact: ~80KB**

**Current state:** 4 admin pages served as raw bytes:
- `admin-users.html` (928 lines)
- `admin-mcp.html` (579 lines)
- `admin-realtime.html` (529 lines)
- Admin uploads is inline HTML in `main.go` (lines ~5154-5251)

**Required infrastructure changes:**
1. Convert all 3 HTML admin pages to Go template processing (same as Phase 6)
2. The inline HTML admin uploads page (built in Go code) already has access to translations -- just needs the translate calls added
3. Add FuncMap with `translate` to each handler
4. Replace all hardcoded text with translation keys

**Key strings across admin pages:** "Users", "Uploads", "Tracks", "MCP Analytics", "Back to Map", table headers (Email, Username, Created, Role, Actions, etc.), button labels (Delete, Edit, Save), pagination text, error messages, status labels.

**Note:** Admin pages might reasonably stay English-only if the admin audience is always English-speaking. This is a judgment call.

---

### Phase 9: MCP Server Tool Descriptions (PR 9 -- DEPRIORITIZE or SKIP)
**Priority: LOW -- these are consumed by AI models, not humans directly**
**Estimated strings: ~90 across 17 tool files**

**Current state:** Tool descriptions are hardcoded Go string constants like:
```go
mcp.WithDescription("Find radiation measurements within a geographic bounding box...")
```

**Challenge:** MCP protocol does not include a language negotiation mechanism. The tool descriptions are sent once during the MCP `initialize` handshake.

**Recommendation: SKIP this phase.** MCP tool descriptions are consumed by AI models (Claude, GPT, etc.) that already understand English. Translating these provides no user benefit and adds significant complexity. If needed in the future, a `-mcp-lang` flag could be added to set the language at startup.

---

### Phase 10: Translation File Optimization (PR 10 -- optional, do if file size becomes a problem)
**Priority: LOW -- optimization**

After all phases, `translations.json` will be approximately:
- Current: 427KB
- Phase 1: +25KB = 452KB
- Phase 2: +196KB = 648KB
- Phases 3-8: +175KB = 823KB

**Potential optimizations if 823KB becomes problematic:**
1. **Split by category:** Move `legend_full_*` keys to `translations-legend.json` (loaded separately)
2. **Lazy load by language:** Instead of sending ALL translations to the client, only send the detected language + English fallback
3. **Compression:** The JSON file compresses very well with gzip (typically 4:1 ratio), so the wire transfer is ~200KB even without splitting

**Recommendation:** Implement lazy language loading in Phase 3 or later. Change `TranslationsJSON` to only include `translations[lang]` and `translations["en"]` instead of the full map. This is a one-line change in each handler:

```go
clientTranslations := map[string]map[string]string{
    "en": translations["en"],
    lang: translations[lang],
}
translationsJSON, err := marshalTemplateJS(clientTranslations)
```

This cuts client-side payload from 823KB to ~57KB (2 languages * ~28KB each).

---

### Implementation Sequencing

```
Phase 1 (content only, no code) -----> can start immediately
Phase 2 (content only, no code) -----> can start immediately, parallel with Phase 1
Phase 3 (AI widget, map.html JS) ----> requires Phase 1/2 merge for clean diff
Phase 4 (auth modals, map.html) -----> can parallel with Phase 3
Phase 5 (search/spectrum, map.html) -> can parallel with Phase 3/4
Phase 6 (profile/reset infra) -------> independent, any time
Phase 7 (web-chat, separate binary) -> independent, any time
Phase 8 (admin pages) ---------------> last, lowest priority
Phase 10 (optimization) -------------> do alongside Phase 3+
```

**Recommended PR order:**
1. Phase 10 (optimization) + Phase 1 -- lazy language loading + fix incomplete translations
2. Phase 2 -- legend expansion (large content PR, review separately)
3. Phase 3 + Phase 4 + Phase 5 -- all map.html JS/HTML i18n in one or separate PRs
4. Phase 6 -- profile/reset template infrastructure
5. Phase 7 -- web-chat i18n
6. Phase 8 -- admin pages (optional)

### Critical Files for Implementation
- `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/public_html/translations.json` - Central translation file; every phase modifies this
- `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/public_html/map.html` - Contains AI widget, auth modals, search bar, spectrum modals -- Phases 3-5
- `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/main.go` - Go handlers need template processing changes for profile/reset/admin -- Phases 6, 8, and the lazy loading optimization
- `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/web-chat/main.go` - Needs template processing and translation loading -- Phase 7
- `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/public_html/profile.html` - Needs conversion from raw bytes to Go template -- Phase 6

## Approved Plan:
# Complete i18n for Safecast New Map

## Context
The map UI has partial i18n (translations.json with 137 keys, 29 languages) but many areas have hardcoded English: the AI assistant widget, login/register modals, search bar, spectrum modals, profile page, admin pages, and the standalone web-chat. Additionally, legal/license/sources content is incomplete for zh/ja/ko, and the radiation safety legend is a detailed 7K-char HTML guide in English but only 300-700 char stubs in all other languages.

## Plan Overview

### PR 1: Fix incomplete legal/license/sources + expand legend (content-only)
**Files:** `cmd/unified-server/public_html/translations.json`, `public_html/translations.json`

1. Expand `legal_full` for zh, ja, ko to match English detail
2. Expand `license_full` for zh
3. Add/expand `sources_full` for ja, ko
4. Expand all 28 `legend_full_XX` keys to match `legend_full_en` detail level with proper HTML formatting (~7K chars each)

### PR 2: AI Assistant widget + auth modals + search/spectrum i18n (map.html)
**Files:** `translations.json`, `cmd/unified-server/public_html/map.html`

New translation keys (~70):
- **AI widget (~15):** `ai_title`, `ai_subtitle`, `ai_placeholder`, `ai_welcome`, `ai_thinking`, `ai_disclaimer`, `ai_error_prefix`, `ai_connection_error`, `ai_expand`, `ai_collapse`, `ai_close`, `ai_open`, `ai_send`, `ai_search_map`
- **Auth modals (~30):** `auth_login_title`, `auth_email_label`, `auth_password_label`, `auth_login_button`, `auth_register_title`, `auth_register_button`, `auth_reset_title`, `auth_reset_button`, `auth_forgot_link`, `auth_logout_button`, etc.
- **Search/spectrum (~25):** `search_placeholder`, `spectrum_title`, `spectrum_download_json`, `spectrum_download_csv`, `spectrum_location_title`, etc.

Replace hardcoded strings with `translate('key')` in JS and `{{translate "key"}}` in template HTML.

### PR 3: Profile page + reset-password i18n
**Files:** `cmd/unified-server/main.go`, `cmd/unified-server/public_html/profile.html`

1. Convert profile handler from raw `ReadFile`+`Write` to Go template processing (same pattern as map.html handler)
2. Add `translate` FuncMap, pass `Lang` and `TranslationsJSON`
3. Replace ~25 hardcoded strings with translate calls
4. Same for reset-password.html (~10 strings)

### PR 4: Web-chat standalone i18n
**Files:** `cmd/web-chat/main.go`, `cmd/web-chat/index.html`, new `cmd/web-chat/translations.json`

1. Create small `cmd/web-chat/translations.json` with ~20 keys for 29 languages
2. Add `getPreferredLanguage()` and template processing to web-chat main.go
3. Replace ~20 hardcoded strings (title, header, suggestion chips, disclaimer, error messages)

### PR 5: Admin pages i18n
**Files:** `cmd/unified-server/main.go`, `admin-users.html`, `admin-mcp.html`, `admin-realtime.html`

1. Convert 3 admin handlers from raw bytes to Go template processing
2. Add ~100 translation keys across all admin pages
3. Replace hardcoded table headers, buttons, pagination text, status labels, error messages

### PR 6: MCP server tool descriptions
**Files:** `cmd/mcp-server/tool_*.go`, `cmd/mcp-server/main.go`

1. Add `-mcp-lang` startup flag (default: en)
2. Load translations at startup, apply to tool descriptions
3. ~90 strings across 17 tool files

### Optimization (apply in PR 2)
Change `TranslationsJSON` to only send current language + English fallback instead of all 29 languages. Cuts client payload from ~800KB to ~57KB.

```go
clientTranslations := map[string]map[string]string{
    "en": translations["en"],
    lang: translations[lang],
}
```

## Verification
- After each PR: rebuild with `/usr/local/go/bin/go build -o safecast-new-map ./cmd/unified-server/`
- Test with `?lang=cs`, `?lang=ja`, `?lang=zh` to verify translations appear
- Check that English fallback works for any missing keys
- For web-chat: rebuild with `go build -o safecast-web-chat ./cmd/web-chat/` and test

```

Preamble. We are building an open map where people around the globe share dosimeter readings for the common good — science, ecology, education, and safety. By publishing your data you help many. Please treat this shared work with care.

1) Responsibility. The accuracy and content of any information you submit remain your responsibility. Data are published and used “as is”. The service does not confirm or guarantee their correctness, completeness, or suitability for any purpose and bears no responsibility for any possible outcome of their use.

2) Openness and license. By sharing dosimetry readings, dates, approximate locations, device models, or other facts, you understand they become available to everyone and may be used freely under the CC0 1.0 (Public Domain) license for data, while the code remains available under the MIT license. Authorship stays with you; no compensation is provided; further redistribution by third parties is beyond our control.

3) “As is” and no verification. Publications appear without prior review. We cannot guarantee instrument calibration or the absence of mistakes. The information is shared for research and is not a professional recommendation.

4) Privacy and moderation. To keep the map safe and trustworthy, timestamps and coordinates may be generalized, and technical metadata may be removed or anonymized. We may hide or delete materials that, in our reasonable opinion, are spam, falsified, unlawful, or disruptive. We handle bona fide measurements with care and strive to preserve them.

5) Cookies. The site relies on a short-lived technical session cookie; it disappears when your visit ends. We do not keep any other traces.

Friends, this map is the result of shared effort and open hearts. Please treat it as a sketch of the terrain, not a precise blueprint. If our work resonates with you, join in — together we can make it better.

gap:12px;"><div style="flex:1;"><h3 style="margin:4px 0;">The most dangerous routes are quiet and long</h3><ul style="margin:4px 0 0 18px;"><li><strong>Through breathing</strong> — radon, dust, smoke → lungs</li><li><strong>Through water</strong> — tritium and dissolved isotopes → whole body</li><li><strong>Through food</strong> — mushrooms, greens, milk → accumulation in tissues</li><li><strong>Through smoking</strong> — tobacco readily absorbs radionuclides</li></ul></div><div style="flex:1;"><h3 style="margin:4px 0;">Less dangerous:</h3><ul style="margin:4px 0 0 18px;"><li>brief external exposure</li><li>one-off contact</li></ul></div></div></section><hr /><section style="margin:0 0 12px;"><h2>⏳ Time is the main risk factor</h2><p style="margin:4px 0;">Radiation is dangerous <strong>because of prolonged exposure</strong>. Moderate doses taken continuously can be more harmful than a short spike. The body can eliminate radionuclides, but with constant exposure and other stresses those systems <strong>get overloaded</strong>.</p></section><hr /><section style="margin:0 0 12px;"><h2>👥 Who is affected more severely</h2><p style="margin:4px 0;">Higher risk for: older people; those with chronic illnesses. What one body tolerates without consequences may cause serious problems for another.</p></section><hr /><section style="margin:0 0 12px;"><h2>⚛️ Radioactive "disguise"</h2><p style="margin:4px 0;">Some radionuclides resemble ordinary substances to the body: <strong>tritium</strong> — like water; <strong>carbon-14</strong> — like normal carbon; <strong>potassium-40</strong> — like potassium; <strong>strontium, radium</strong> — like calcium. Cells <strong>do not recognise the substitution</strong> and incorporate them. Damage and mutations are possible even when the dosimeter reads "normal."</p></section><hr /><section style="margin:0;"><h2>🗺️ Why a map should not be trusted blindly</h2><p style="margin:4px 0;">Even a perfectly green map reflects <strong>only part of reality</strong>. Hidden factors are not shown. 👉 A map is a hint, not a pardon. You still need to think, check, and ask questions yourself.</p></section>

Организм способен выводить радионуклиды, но при постоянном облучении и других нагрузках эти системы <strong>перегружаются</strong>.</p></section><hr /><section style=\"margin:0 0 12px;\"><h2>👥 Кто подвергается большему риску</h2><p style=\"margin:4px 0;\">Повышенный риск: пожилые люди; люди с хроническими заболеваниями. ## Database Patterns Analysis - Safecast Project

I've thoroughly explored the database architecture and patterns in this project. Here's a comprehensive report:

---

## 1. MIGRATION HANDLING

**Location:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/migrations/`

**Migration Files:**
- `/migrations/add_user_id_column.sql` - Adds user_id tracking to uploads
- `/migrations/add_is_admin_column.sql` - Adds is_admin flag to users
- `/migrations/add_username_column.sql` - Adds username to users
- `/migrations/link_historical_uploads_to_users.sql` - Links existing uploads to user accounts

**Pattern:** SQL files are run manually via `psql`:
```bash
psql -h 127.0.0.1 -U postgres -d safecast -f migrations/filename.sql
```

**Schema Creation:** Schema is built into the binary at `/pkg/database/database.go:998` in the `InitSchema()` function, which runs on startup for both SQLite and PostgreSQL. This is called from main.go line 8589:
```go
if err = db.InitSchema(dbCfg); err != nil {
    log.Fatalf("DB schema: %v", err)
}
```

---

## 2. DATABASE CONNECTION PATTERN

**Global Database Variable:**
- **File:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/main.go:359`
- **Declaration:** `var db *database.Database`
- **Type:** `*database.Database` from `safecast-new-map/pkg/database`

**Initialization:**
- **Location:** `cmd/unified-server/main.go:8580`
```go
db, err = database.NewDatabase(dbCfg)
if err != nil {
    log.Fatalf("DB init: %v", err)
}
```

**Structure:**
- `Database.DB` - Underlying `*sql.DB` connection pool
- `Database.Driver` - Normalized driver name (pgx, sqlite, duckdb)
- Supports multi-driver: PostgreSQL (pgx), SQLite, DuckDB, ClickHouse

**Access Pattern:** Global variable is passed to handlers and auth manager:
```go
authManager = &auth.Manager{
    DB:                db.DB,      // Pass the sql.DB connection
    DBDriver:          driverName,  // Pass normalized driver name
    ...
}
```

---

## 3. DATABASE TABLES & SCHEMA

**Users Table** (`/pkg/database/database.go:1133-1148`):
```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS users (
  id              BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
  email           TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL,
  password_hash   TEXT NOT NULL,
  username        VARCHAR(50) UNIQUE,
  email_verified  BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE,
  created_at      TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
  updated_at      TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
  last_login_at   TIMESTAMPTZ,
  is_active       BOOLEAN DEFAULT TRUE,
  is_admin        BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE,
  external_id     TEXT,
  external_source TEXT,
  requires_password_setup BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE,
  api_key         TEXT
);
```

**Uploads Table** (`/pkg/database/database.go:1108-1124`):
```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS uploads (
  id              BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
  filename        TEXT NOT NULL,
  file_type       TEXT,
  track_id        TEXT,
  file_size       BIGINT,
  upload_ip       TEXT,
  created_at      TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
  recording_date  TIMESTAMPTZ,
  source          TEXT,
  source_id       TEXT,
  source_url      TEXT,
  user_id         TEXT,
  username        TEXT,
  internal_user_id TEXT,    -- Links to users.id
  detector        TEXT
);
```

**Supporting Tables:**
- `sessions` - User sessions with cascading deletes to users
- `password_reset_tokens` - Password reset with expiry
- `email_verification_tokens` - Email verification tokens
- `markers`, `tracks`, `realtime_measurements`, `spectra`, `short_links`

---

## 4. ADMIN CRUD INTERFACE - USER MANAGEMENT

**Frontend HTML:** `/home/rob/Documents/Safecast/safecast-new-map/cmd/unified-server/public_html/admin-users.html`

**Key Frontend Features:**
- Pagination with configurable page size (25, 50, 100, 200)
- Client-side sorting on all columns
- Client-side filtering per column
- Search across email, username, ID, external_id
- Modal-based add/edit form
- Bulk delete with checkboxes
- Admin role toggle buttons
- Password reset email trigger
- API key display

**JavaScript Patterns:**
- `loadUsers()` - Fetches paginated data from `/api/admin/users`
- `saveUser(event)` - POST to `/api/admin/users/create` or PUT to `/api/admin/users/{id}`
- `deleteUser(userId)` - DELETE to `/api/admin/users/{id}`
- `toggleAdmin(userId, makeAdmin)` - PUT to `/api/admin/users/{id}` with `{is_admin: boolean}`
- `resetUserPassword(userId)` - POST to `/api/admin/users/{id}/reset-password`

---

## 5. GO HANDLER ARCHITECTURE

**Registration Pattern:** `/pkg/httpapi/register.go:73-105`

```go
mux.HandleFunc("/api/admin/users", cfg.AuthManager.OptionalAuth(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
    if !checkAdminAccess(w, r, cfg.AdminPassword) {
        return
    }
    cfg.AuthManager.AdminListUsersHandler(w, r)
}))

mux.HandleFunc("/api/admin/users/create", cfg.AuthManager.OptionalAuth(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
    if !checkAdminAccess(w, r, cfg.AdminPassword) {
        return
    }
    cfg.AuthManager.AdminCreateUserHandler(w, r)
}))

mux.HandleFunc("/api/admin/users/", cfg.AuthManager.OptionalAuth(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
    if !checkAdminAccess(w, r, cfg.AdminPassword) {
        return
    }
    switch r.Method {
    case http.MethodPut, http.MethodPatch:
        cfg.AuthManager.AdminUpdateUserHandler(w, r)
    case http.MethodDelete:
        cfg.AuthManager.AdminDeleteUserHandler(w, r)
    case http.MethodPost:
        if strings.HasSuffix(r.URL.Path, "/reset-password") {
            cfg.AuthManager.AdminResetUserPasswordHandler(w, r)
        } else if strings.HasSuffix(r.URL.Path, "/regenerate-api-key") {
            cfg.AuthManager.AdminRegenerateAPIKeyHandler(w, r)
        }
    }
}))
```

**Handler Methods in Auth Manager:** `/pkg/auth/admin_handlers.go`

| Handler | Method | Purpose | File |
|---------|--------|---------|------|
| `AdminListUsersHandler` | GET `/api/admin/users` | List with pagination & search | Line 17 |
| `AdminCreateUserHandler` | POST `/api/admin/users/create` | Create new user | Line 64 |
| `AdminUpdateUserHandler` | PUT/PATCH `/api/admin/users/{id}` | Update user fields | Line 179 |
| `AdminDeleteUserHandler` | DELETE `/api/admin/users/{id}` | Delete user | Line 272 |
| `AdminResetUserPasswordHandler` | POST `/api/admin/users/{id}/reset-password` | Send reset email | Line 302 |
| `AdminRegenerateAPIKeyHandler` | POST `/api/admin/users/{id}/regenerate-api-key` | Regenerate API key | Line 373 |

---

## 6. DATABASE QUERY FUNCTIONS

**Location:** `/pkg/auth/database.go` (968 lines total)

**User CRUD Functions:**

| Function | Location | Purpose |
|----------|----------|---------|
| `CreateUser()` | Line 31 | Insert new user with hashed password |
| `GetUserByID()` | Line 179 | Fetch user by ID |
| `GetUserByEmail()` | Line 115 | Fetch user by email |
| `UpdateUser()` | Line 716 | Update user fields |
| `DeleteUser()` | Line 770 | Delete user (cascades to sessions/tokens) |
| `GetUsersPaginated()` | Line 788 | List with limit/offset/search |
| `CountUsers()` | Line 884 | Count total users (for pagination) |

**Key Implementation Details:**

1. **Multi-Driver Support:**
   - PostgreSQL (pgx): Uses `$1, $2...` placeholders, `EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM ...)` for timestamps, `ILIKE` for search
   - SQLite/Chai: Uses `?` placeholders, stores timestamps as integers
   - Automatic boolean conversion for SQLite (0/1)

2. **Pagination Pattern** (GetUsersPaginated):
   ```go
   func GetUsersPaginated(ctx context.Context, db *sql.DB, dbDriver string, limit, offset int, search string) ([]*User, error) {
       // Validates limit (default 50)
       // Builds driver-specific WHERE clause with ILIKE or LIKE
       // Searches: email, username, external_id, ID as text
       // Returns []*User slice
   }
   ```

3. **Search Pattern:**
   - Email: `ILIKE` (PostgreSQL) or `LIKE` (SQLite) with `%search%`
   - Username, external_id, ID: Same LIKE pattern
   - All fields optional in search

4. **Update Pattern:**
   - Takes pointer to `User` struct
   - Updates: email, username, email_verified, is_active, is_admin, external_id, external_source, requires_password_setup
   - Does NOT update: password_hash (separate function), api_key (separate function)
   - Sets `updated_at` to current time automatically

---

## 7. AUTH MANAGER PATTERN

**Location:** `/pkg/auth/manager.go` (implied from usage)

**Manager Structure** (from register.go):
```go
authManager = &auth.Manager{
    DB:                db.DB,              // *sql.DB connection pool
    DBDriver:          driverName,         // "pgx", "sqlite", etc.
    SessionCookieName: *sessionCookieName,
    AllowRegistration: *allowRegistration,
    EmailSender:       emailSender,        // For password resets, etc.
    BaseURL:           *baseURL,           // For email links
}
```

**Key Methods:**
- `OptionalAuth()` - Middleware: continues if user logged in OR can proceed without auth
- `RequireAuth()` - Middleware: requires authenticated user
- `AdminListUsersHandler()`, `AdminCreateUserHandler()`, etc. (6 admin handlers)

**Authentication Flow:**
1. Session-based: Check `authManager.ProfileHandler()` via `/api/user/profile`
2. Password-based: Check `?password=xxx` query parameter
3. Both checked by `checkAdminAccess()` wrapper in register.go

---

## 8. ADMIN HANDLER EXAMPLES

**MCP Analytics Handler** (similar pattern for admin tables):
- **File:** `/cmd/unified-server/admin_mcp.go:15`
- **Type:** Table data handler with pagination, sorting, search
- **Query Pattern:**
  ```go
  // 1. Validate table name against whitelist
  // 2. Parse limit (max 1000), offset, sort column, order, search
  // 3. Build WHERE clause dynamically for search
  // 4. Count total: SELECT COUNT(*) FROM table WHERE ...
  // 5. Fetch data: SELECT cols FROM table WHERE ... ORDER BY ... LIMIT OFFSET
  // 6. Return JSON with data + total for pagination
  ```

**Realtime Handler:** `/cmd/unified-server/admin_realtime.go:28` - Similar pattern

---

## 9. KEY PATTERNS FOR TRANSLATIONS TABLE

### Suggested Architecture:

**1. Migration File:** `/migrations/add_translations_table.sql`
```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS translations (
  id              BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
  language_code   VARCHAR(10) NOT NULL,
  key             VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
  value           TEXT NOT NULL,
  created_at      TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
  updated_at      TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
  created_by      BIGINT REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
  CONSTRAINT translations_unique UNIQUE (language_code, key)
);
CREATE INDEX idx_translations_lang ON translations(language_code);
```

**2. Go Model:** In `/pkg/auth/models.go` or new `/pkg/translation/models.go`
```go
type Translation struct {
    ID           int64  `json:"id"`
    LanguageCode string `json:"language_code"`
    Key          string `json:"key"`
    Value        string `json:"value"`
    CreatedAt    int64  `json:"created_at"`
    UpdatedAt    int64  `json:"updated_at"`
    CreatedBy    int64  `json:"created_by,omitempty"`
}
```

**3. Database Functions:** In `/pkg/translation/database.go`
- `CreateTranslation()` - Insert new translation
- `GetTranslation()` - Get by language + key
- `GetTranslationsByLanguage()` - Get all for language
- `UpdateTranslation()` - Update value
- `DeleteTranslation()` - Delete entry
- `GetTranslationsPaginated()` - For admin listing

**4. Admin Handlers:** In `/pkg/translation/admin_handlers.go` or new file
- Reuse pattern from `admin_handlers.go`:
  - List with pagination/search
  - Create new translation
  - Update translation
  - Delete translation

**5. Routes:** Register in `/pkg/httpapi/register.go`
```go
mux.HandleFunc("/api/admin/translations", cfg.AuthManager.OptionalAuth(func(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
    if !checkAdminAccess(w, r, cfg.AdminPassword) {
        return
    }
    // List handler
}))
```

**6. Admin Frontend:** `/cmd/unified-server/public_html/admin-translations.html`
- Reuse admin-users.html structure
- Columns: ID, Language, Key, Value, Created By, Created At, Updated At, Actions

---

## 10. KEY FILES SUMMARY

| Path | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `/pkg/database/database.go` | DB init, schema creation, table definitions |
| `/pkg/auth/models.go` | User, Session, Token struct definitions |
| `/pkg/auth/database.go` | All user CRUD functions, pagination logic |
| `/pkg/auth/admin_handlers.go` | 6 admin user management handlers |
| `/pkg/httpapi/register.go` | Route registration, admin route setup |
| `/cmd/unified-server/main.go` | App entry point, DB/auth initialization |
| `/cmd/unified-server/public_html/admin-users.html` | Admin user UI with full CRUD |
| `/migrations/` | SQL migration files (manual execution) |

---

## 11. CRITICAL PATTERNS

1. **Driver-Agnostic SQL:** All queries use switch statements for pgx vs sqlite placeholders
2. **Global DB Access:** Single global `var db *database.Database` in main, passed as `db.DB` and `dbDriver` to handlers
3. **Context Propagation:** All database calls use `ctx context.Context` as first param
4. **Error Handling:** `sql.ErrNoRows` checked specifically, constraint errors for uniqueness
5. **Pagination:** Validated limit/offset, ordered by created_at DESC, returns slice + count
6. **Admin Auth:** Double-wrapped - middleware checks optional auth, handler checks admin flag or password
7. **Timestamps:** UNIX seconds in Go, stored as TIMESTAMPTZ in PostgreSQL (with EXTRACT), as unix seconds in SQLite

This architecture is well-suited for adding a translations table with full admin CRUD functionality!

## Approved Plan:
# Move Translations to PostgreSQL with Admin UI

## Context
Translations are currently in a 427KB JSON file embedded in the binary. Every fix requires a rebuild and redeploy. Moving them to PostgreSQL makes maintenance much easier — an admin page lets contributors edit translations directly, no developer needed.

## Approach

### Step 1: Database table + migration
**File:** `migrations/add_translations_table.sql`

```sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS translations (
  id              BIGSERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
  language_code   VARCHAR(10) NOT NULL,
  key             VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
  value           TEXT NOT NULL,
  updated_at      TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
  CONSTRAINT translations_unique UNIQUE (language_code, key)
);
CREATE INDEX idx_translations_lang ON translations(language_code);
```

### Step 2: Seed the table from existing translations.json
**File:** `migrations/seed_translations.sql` (generated from translations.json by a script)

Import all existing ~137 × 29 = ~3,970 rows from translations.json into the new table. This is a one-time operation.

### Step 3: Load translations from DB at startup
**File:** `cmd/unified-server/main.go`

Replace `loadTranslations(content, "public_html/translations.json")` with a new `loadTranslationsFromDB()` function that:
1. `SELECT language_code, key, value FROM translations`
2. Populates the same `var translations map[string]map[string]string`
3. Falls back to embedded JSON if DB is unavailable (for local dev without DB)

The rest of the code (template FuncMap, `translate()`, `TranslationsJSON`) stays unchanged — it all reads from the same `translations` map.

### Step 4: Admin CRUD API
**File:** new `cmd/unified-server/admin_translations.go`

Following the existing admin pattern (admin_mcp.go, admin_realtime.go):
- `GET /api/admin/translations` — list with pagination, search, filter by language
- `PUT /api/admin/translations/{id}` — update a translation value
- `POST /api/admin/translations` — create new translation
- `DELETE /api/admin/translations/{id}` — delete a translation
- `POST /api/admin/translations/reload` — reload translations into memory (so changes take effect without restart)

Register routes in `pkg/httpapi/register.go`.

### Step 5: Admin UI page
**File:** new `cmd/unified-server/public_html/admin-translations.html`

Based on admin-users.html pattern:
- Table with columns: Language, Key, Value (truncated), Updated At, Actions
- Filter by language dropdown (29 languages)
- Search across key and value
- Inline edit or modal edit for value (with textarea for long content like legal_full)
- Pagination (25/50/100 per page)
- Add new translation button
- Tab added to existing admin nav: "Users | Uploads | MCP Analytics | Realtime | **Translations**"

### Step 6: Add translations tab to admin nav
**Files:** `admin-users.html`, `admin-mcp.html`, `admin-realtime.html`

Add "Translations" link to the admin tabs bar in each file.

### Step 7: Now add all the missing translations
With the admin UI in place, expand content directly in the DB:
- Expand legend_full_XX for all 28 non-English languages
- Add all missing UI strings (AI widget, auth modals, search, profile, admin pages, web-chat, MCP tools)
- This can be done through the admin UI or via SQL scripts

## Key files to modify
- `cmd/unified-server/main.go` — replace `loadTranslations()`, add reload endpoint
- `pkg/httpapi/register.go` — register new admin routes
- `pkg/database/database.go` — add translations table to `InitSchema()`
- New: `cmd/unified-server/admin_translations.go` — CRUD handlers
- New: `cmd/unified-server/public_html/admin-translations.html` — admin UI
- New: `migrations/add_translations_table.sql`
- `admin-users.html`, `admin-mcp.html`, `admin-realtime.html` — add Translations tab

## What stays the same
- `var translations map[string]map[string]string` — same in-memory structure
- `translate()` JS function — unchanged
- `{{translate "key"}}` Go template usage — unchanged
- `TranslationsJSON` — unchanged
- `getPreferredLanguage()` — unchanged
- `?lang=` URL override — unchanged

## Verification
1. Run migration on local DB
2. Seed translations from JSON
3. Rebuild and start server
4. Verify map loads with translations from DB
5. Test admin page: list, filter by language, edit a value, reload
6. Verify `?lang=cs` shows Czech translations
7. Edit a translation in admin, click reload, verify change appears on map

```

Step 2: Generate the seed SQL from translations.json.

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_01GEGPb84zydR6fpv2xFZo1p
```json
{
  "command": "python3 << 'PYEOF'\nimport json\n\nwith open('cmd/unified-server/public_html/translations.json', 'r') as f:\n    data = json.load(f)\n\nlines = [\"-- Seed translations from translations.json\", \n         \"-- Generated automatically - run after add_translations_table.sql\",\n         \"\",\n         \"BEGIN;\",\n         \"\"]\n\ncount = 0\nfor lang in sorted(data.keys()):\n    for key in sorted(data[lang].keys()):\n        value = data[lang][key].replace(\"'\", \"''\")\n        lines.append(f\"INSERT INTO translations (language_code, key, value) VALUES ('{lang}', '{key}', '{value}') ON CONFLICT (language_code, key) DO UPDATE SET value = EXCLUDED.value, updated_at = NOW();\")\n        count += 1\n\nlines.append(\"\")\nlines.append(\"COMMIT;\")\n\nwith open('migrations/seed_translations.sql', 'w') as f:\n    f.write('\\n'.join(lines))\n\nprint(f\"Generated {count} INSERT statements\")\nPYEOF\n",
  "description": "Generate seed SQL from translations.json"
}
```

false\n\t}\n\n\ttranslationsMu.Lock()\n\ttranslations = newTranslations\n\ttranslationsMu.Unlock()\n\n\tlog.Printf(\"[i18n] Loaded %d translations from database\", count)\n\treturn true\n}\n\n// loadTranslationsFromFile loads translations from the embedded JSON file (fallback).\nfunc loadTranslationsFromFile(fs embed.FS, filename string) {\n\tfile, err := fs.Open(filename)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Fatalf(\"Error opening translation file: %v\", err)\n\t}\n\tdefer file.Close()\n\n\tdata, err := ioutil.ReadAll(file)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Fatalf(\"Error reading translation file: == nil {\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tvar count int\n\terr := db.DB.QueryRow(\"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM translations\").Scan(&count)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Printf(\"[i18n] Cannot check translations table: translation file for seeding: %v\", err)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tdefer file.Close()\n\n\tdata, err := ioutil.ReadAll(file)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Printf(\"[i18n] Cannot read translation file for seeding: %v\", err)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tvar fileTranslations map[string]map[string]string\n\tif err := json.Unmarshal(data, &fileTranslations); err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Printf(\"[i18n] Cannot parse translation file for seeding: %v\", err)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\ttx, err := db.DB.Begin()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Printf(\"[i18n] Cannot begin transaction for seeding: %v\", err)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tinserted := 0\n\tfor lang, keys := range fileTranslations {\n\t\tfor key, value := range keys {\n\t\t\t_, err := tx.Exec(\n\t\t\t\t\"INSERT INTO translations (language_code, key, value) VALUES ($1, $2, $3) ON CONFLICT (language_code, key) DO NOTHING\",\n\t\t\t\tlang, key, value,\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\tlog.Printf(\"[i18n] Error seeding %s.%s: %v\", lang, key, err)\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tinserted++\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tif err := tx.Commit(); err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Printf(\"[i18n] Error committing seed transaction: %v\", err)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tlog.Printf(\"[i18n] Seeded %d translations into database from embedded file\", inserted)\n}"
}

    13→// realtimeColumns defines the valid columns for the realtime_measurements table.
    14→var realtimeColumns = []string{
    15→	"id", "device_id", "device_name", "tube", "transport", "country",
    16→	"value", "unit", "lat", "lon", "measured_at", "fetched_at", "extra",
    17→}
    18→
    19→// latestPerDeviceCTE returns a CTE that selects only the most recent row per device_id.
    20→const latestPerDeviceCTE = `WITH latest AS (
    21→  SELECT DISTINCT ON (device_id) ` + "id, device_id, device_name, tube, transport, country, value, unit, lat, lon, measured_at, fetched_at, extra" + `
    22→  FROM realtime_measurements
    23→  ORDER BY device_id, fetched_at DESC
    24→)`
    25→
    26→// adminRealtimeDataHandler returns JSON data for the latest reading per device.
    27→// GET /api/admin/realtime/data?limit=50&offset=0&sort=fetched_at&order=desc&search=...
    28→func adminRealtimeDataHandler(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {
    29→	if db == nil {
    30→		http.Error(w, "Database not available", http.StatusServiceUnavailable)
    31→		return
    32→	}
    33→
    34→	limit, _ := strconv.Atoi(r.URL.Query().Get("limit"))
    35→	if limit <= 0 || limit > 1000 {
    36→		limit = 50
    37→	}
    38→	offset, _ := strconv.Atoi(r.URL.Query().Get("offset"))
    39→	if offset < 0 {
    40→		offset = 0
    41→	}
    42→
    43→	sortCol := r.URL.Query().Get("sort")
    44→	if !isValidColumn(sortCol, realtimeColumns) {
    45→		sortCol = "fetched_at"
    46→	}
    47→	order := strings.ToUpper(r.URL.Query().Get("order"))
    48→	if order != "ASC" {
    49→		order = "DESC"
    50→	}
    51→
    52→	search := r.URL.Query().Get("search")
    53→	whereSQL, args := realtimeSearchWhere(search)
    54→
    55→	// Count total (latest per device, then filter)
    56→	countQuery := fmt.Sprintf("%s SELECT COUNT(*) FROM latest %s", latestPerDeviceCTE, whereSQL)
    57→	var total int
    58→	if err := db.DB.QueryRow(countQuery, args...).Scan(&total); err != nil {
    59→		log.Printf("admin realtime count error: %v", err)
    60→		w.Header().Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
    61→		w.WriteHeader(http.StatusInternalServerError)
    62→		json.NewEncoder(w).Encode(map[string]interface{}{
    63→			"error": fmt.Sprintf("Query failed: %v", err),
    64→			"data":  []interface{}{},
    65→			"total": 0,
    66→		})
    67→		return
    68→	}
    69→
    70→	// Fetch data
    71→	colList := strings.Join(realtimeColumns, ", ")
    72→	dataQuery := fmt.Sprintf("%s SELECT %s FROM latest %s ORDER BY %s %s LIMIT %d OFFSET %d",
    73→		latestPerDeviceCTE, colList, whereSQL, sortCol, order, limit, offset)
    74→
    75→	rows, err := db.DB.Query(dataQuery, args...)
    76→	if err != nil {
    77→		log.Printf("admin realtime query error: %v", err)
    78→		http.Error(w, "Query failed", http.StatusInternalServerError)
    79→		return
    80→	}
    81→	defer rows.Close()
    82→
    83→	var results []map[string]interface{}
    84→	for rows.Next() {
    85→		values := make([]interface{}, len(realtimeColumns))
    86→		ptrs := make([]interface{}, len(realtimeColumns))
    87→		for i := range values {
    88→			ptrs[i] = &values[i]
    89→		}
    90→		if err := rows.Scan(ptrs...); err != nil {
    91→			log.Printf("admin realtime scan error: %v", err)
    92→			continue
    93→		}
    94→		row := make(map[string]interface{})
    95→		for i, col := range realtimeColumns {
    96→			if b, ok := values[i].([]byte); ok {
    97→				row[col] = string(b)
    98→			} else {
    99→				row[col] = values[i]
   100→			}

{\n\t\tlimit = 50\n\t}\n\toffset, _ := strconv.Atoi(r.URL.Query().Get(\"offset\"))\n\tif offset < 0 {\n\t\toffset = 0\n\t}\n\n\tsortCol := r.URL.Query().Get(\"sort\")\n\tif !isValidColumn(sortCol, translationColumns) {\n\t\tsortCol = \"key\"\n\t}\n\torder := strings.ToUpper(r.URL.Query().Get(\"order\"))\n\tif order != \"DESC\" {\n\t\torder = \"ASC\"\n\t}\n\n\tsearch := r.URL.Query().Get(\"search\")\n\tlangFilter := r.URL.Query().Get(\"lang\")\n\n\t// Build WHERE clause\n\tvar conditions []string\n\tvar args []interface{}\n\targIdx := 1\n\n\tif langFilter != \"\" json.NewDecoder(r.Body).Decode(&payload); err != nil {\n\t\thttp.Error(w, \"Invalid JSON\", http.StatusBadRequest)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tif payload.LanguageCode == \"\" || payload.Key == \"\" {\n\t\thttp.Error(w, \"language_code and key are required\", http.StatusBadRequest)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\t_, err := db.DB.Exec(\n\t\t\"INSERT INTO translations (language_code, key, value) VALUES ($1, $2, $3) ON CONFLICT (language_code, key) DO UPDATE SET value = EXCLUDED.value, updated_at = NOW()\",\n\t\tpayload.LanguageCode, payload.Key, payload.Value,\n\t)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Printf(\"admin translation create error: %v\", err)\n\t\thttp.Error(w, \"Create failed\", http.StatusInternalServerError)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tw.Header().Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n\tw.WriteHeader(http.StatusCreated)\n\tjson.NewEncoder(w).Encode(map[string]string{\"status\": \"ok\"})\n}\n\n// adminTranslationDeleteHandler deletes a translation.\n// DELETE /api/admin/translations/{id}\nfunc adminTranslationDeleteHandler(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\tif db == nil {\n\t\thttp.Error(w, \"Database not available\", http.StatusServiceUnavailable)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tparts := strings.Split(strings.TrimSuffix(r.URL.Path, \"/\"), \"/\")\n\tif len(parts) < 4 {\n\t\thttp.Error(w, \"Missing translation ID\", http.StatusBadRequest)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\tidStr := parts[len(parts)-1]\n\tid, err := strconv.ParseInt(idStr, 10, 64)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\thttp.Error(w, \"Invalid translation ID\", http.StatusBadRequest)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\t_, err = db.DB.Exec(\"DELETE FROM translations WHERE id = $1\", id)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Printf(\"admin translation delete error: %v\", err)\n\t\thttp.Error(w, \"Delete failed\", http.StatusInternalServerError)\n\t\treturn\n\t}\n\n\tw.Header().Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n\tjson.NewEncoder(w).Encode(map[string]string{\"status\": \"ok\"})\n}\n\n// adminTranslationsReloadHandler reloads translations from DB into memory.\n// POST /api/admin/translations/reload\nfunc adminTranslationsReloadHandler(w http.ResponseWriter, r *http.Request) {\n\tif loadTranslationsFromDB() {\n\t\tw.Header().Set(\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\")\n\t\tjson.NewEncoder(w).Encode(map[string]string{\"status\": \"ok\", \"message\": \"Translations reloaded from database\"})\n\t} else {\n\t\thttp.Error(w, \"Failed to reload translations\", http.StatusInternalServerError)\n\t}\n}\n"
}

   154→<script>
